0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{b}}(n)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{le}}(n)\longrightarrow 0

we derive (similarly to the case of Hamiltonian Lie
(super)algebras) the long exact sequence

(212)

\begin{array}[]{l}\ldots\longrightarrow H^{1}({\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{1}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{b}})\longrightarrow H^{1}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{2}({\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{2}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{b}})\longrightarrow\\
\longrightarrow H^{2}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{3}({\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{3}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{b}})\longrightarrow H^{3}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow H^{4}({\mathfrak{le}})\longrightarrow\ldots\end{array}

From (
188
) we have
H^{i}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{b}})=H^{i}({\mathfrak{pe}};\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{pe}}})=0
for
i=1,2,3
. Since
H^{3}({\mathfrak{le}})\simeq\Pi{\mathbb{C}}={\mathbb{C}}[\omega_{1}^{*}\wedge 1_{n}^{*}]
by the same arguments as in the case where
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{h}}
, it follows that
H^{2}({\mathfrak{le}};{\mathfrak{le}})\simeq\Pi{\mathbb{C}}
. Clearly, this deformation of
{\mathfrak{le}}
is the restriction of the quantization of
{\mathfrak{b}}
, see Subsection
2.12
.

{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{sle}}^{\prime}(n)
,
n>2
. The answer is checked via computer for
n
small:
{\mathfrak{sle}}^{\prime}(n)
is rigid. ∎

Conjecture

(Rigidity of exceptional simple vectorial Lie superalgebras)
.

The exceptional simple vectorial Lie
superalgebras are rigid.

10.
Deformations of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}(n;n)

(proof of claims in
[
LSh3
]
)

In the journal where
[
LSh3
]
was published, the technical mathematical details were out of place. Let us give them here. Recall that
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}(n;n)=(\Pi(\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda}),{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|n))_{*}
and the
{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|n)
-action (
47
) on
the space of
\lambda
-densities. For
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}
, the action preserves the
non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form

(a\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits},b\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits})=\int ab\ \mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits=(-1)^{p(a)p(b)}\int ba\ \mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits.

Hence, on
\Pi(\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits})
, the form

(\Pi(a\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}),\Pi(b\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}))=\int ab\ \mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits

is non-degenerate and
anti
-symmetric.

10.1.
If
n=2

Due to an isomorphism
{\mathfrak{osp}}(2|2)\simeq{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|2)
, we deduce that
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(2;2)\simeq{\mathfrak{h}}(2|2)
. The elements
of
{\mathfrak{h}}(2|2)
are given in terms of generating functions as
H_{f}
,
and our first step in the description of the main deformation of

{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}
is a realization of the elements of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}
as deformations of the fields
H_{f}
,
i.e.
, we construct a map

f\longmapsto H_{f}+\hbar(\lambda)W_{f}\in{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}

such that
\hbar(\lambda)\longrightarrow 0
as
\lambda\longrightarrow\frac{1}{2}
and

{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}=\mathop{\mathrm{{Span}}}\nolimits(H_{f}+\hbar(\lambda)W_{f}\mid f\in{\mathcal{F}})
.

Here is the description of the map

{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|2)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{vect}}(2|2)_{0}\simeq{\mathfrak{gl}}(2|2)
. Let a basis in
\Pi(\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda})
be

(213)

\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\alpha\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\beta\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\alpha\beta\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},

i.e.
, as space,
\Pi(\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda})
is isomorphic to the Grassmann algebra generated
by
\alpha
and
\beta
.

Let
{\mathfrak{vect}}(2|2)={\mathfrak{der}}~{\mathbb{C}}[p,q,\xi,\eta]
. Let us identify

\Pi(\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda})
with
{\mathfrak{vect}}(2|2)_{-1}
by setting

(214)

\partial_{\xi}:=\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\partial_{p}:=\alpha\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\partial_{q}:=\beta\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda},\quad\partial_{\eta}:=\alpha\beta\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{\lambda}.

Here is the representation of
{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|2)={\mathfrak{der}}~{\mathbb{C}}[\alpha,\beta]
in terms of
p,q,\xi,\eta
:

(215)

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\alpha}}}&=-p{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}+\eta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}},\\
{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\beta}}}&=-q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}-\eta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}},\\
\alpha{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\beta}}}&=-q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}},\\
\beta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\alpha}}}&=-p{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}},\\
\alpha{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\alpha}}}&=\lambda\xi{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}+(\lambda-1)p{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}}+\lambda q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}}+(\lambda-1)\eta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}},\\
\beta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\beta}}}&=\lambda\xi{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}+\lambda p{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}}+(\lambda-1)q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}}+(\lambda-1)\eta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}},\\
\alpha\beta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\alpha}}}&=\lambda\xi{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}}+(\lambda-1)p{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}},\\
\alpha\beta{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\beta}}}&=-\lambda\xi{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}}-(\lambda-1)q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}.\end{array}

Looking at the explicit basis of
({\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda})_{0}
, we see that the

linear
operator

D=P{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}}+Q{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}}+X{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}+Y{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}\in{\mathfrak{vect}}(2|2)

belongs to
({\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda})_{0}
if it satisfies the
following system of differential equations (here
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}+h
)

(216)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\frac{\partial{X}}{\partial{\eta}}}=0&(1)\\
{\frac{\partial{Y}}{\partial{\xi}}}=0&(2)\\
{\frac{\partial{X}}{\partial{p}}}+(-1)^{p(D)}{\frac{\partial{Q}}{\partial{\eta}}}=0&(3)\\
{\frac{\partial{X}}{\partial{q}}}-(-1)^{p(D)}{\frac{\partial{P}}{\partial{\eta}}}=0&(4)\\
{\frac{\partial{P}}{\partial{p}}}+{\frac{\partial{Q}}{\partial{q}}}=(-1)^{p(D)}({\frac{\partial{X}}{\partial{\xi}}}+{\frac{\partial{Y}}{\partial{\eta}}})&(5)\\
(1+2h){\frac{\partial{Y}}{\partial{p}}}=(2h-1)(-1)^{p(D)}{\frac{\partial{Q}}{\partial{\xi}}}&(6)\\
(1+2h){\frac{\partial{Y}}{\partial{q}}}=-(2h-1)(-1)^{p(D)}{\frac{\partial{P}}{\partial{\xi}}}&(7)\\
(1+2h)({\frac{\partial{P}}{\partial{p}}}+{\frac{\partial{Q}}{\partial{q}}})=4h(-1)^{p(D)}{\frac{\partial{X}}{\partial{\xi}}}&(8)\end{array}\right.

By dimensions considerations, any
linear
operator
D
satisfying conditions
(1)–(8) of (
216
) belongs to
({\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda})_{0}
. By the definition of the Cartan
prolongation,
any

D
satisfying conditions (1)–(8) belongs to

{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}
.

The explicit solution of the system (1)–(8) of (
216
) (here we omit a page of obvious
transformations) is given in terms of one function
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[p,q,\xi,\eta]
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}P=-{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{q}}}+P_{0}(p,q),\\
Q={\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{p}}},\\
X=-(-1)^{p(f)}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\eta}}},\\
Y=(-1)^{p(f)}\frac{2h-1}{1+2h}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}},\\
\end{array}\right.

where

(217)

{\frac{\partial{P_{0}}}{\partial{p}}}=-\frac{4h}{1+2h}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\xi\eta}.

The relation (
217
) determines
P_{0}
up to a term that
depends only on
q
. It remains to observe that adding to
f
such
a term does not affecty the values of
Q
,
X
,
Y
. Therefore, we
can set

(218)

P_{0}=-\frac{4h}{1+2h}\int^{p}_{0}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\xi\eta}dp=\frac{4h}{1+2h}\int^{p}_{0}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\eta\xi}dp.

Now let

\hbar=\frac{4h}{1+2h}=\frac{2\lambda-1}{\lambda}\longrightarrow 0\iff\lambda\longrightarrow\frac{1}{2}.

To summarize:

Theorem

(Deformation of the Buttin bracket)
.

Set

\hbar(\lambda)=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{2\lambda-1}{\lambda}$}}{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{2\lambda-1}{\lambda}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{2\lambda-1}{\lambda}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{2\lambda-1}{\lambda}$}}
.

Then,
D\in{\mathfrak{vect}}(2|2)

belongs to
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\lambda}(2;2)
if and only if

(219)

D=D_{f}:=H_{f}+\hbar(\lambda)W_{f},\text{ where
$W_{f}=\left(\int_{0}^{p}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}dp\right)\partial_{p}+(-1)^{p(f)}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}\partial_{\eta}$}

for some
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[p,q,\xi,\eta]
. Then,

(220)

[D_{f},D_{g}]=D_{\{f,g\}}+\hbar(\lambda)D_{c(f,g)}\text{~~for any $f,g\in{\mathbb{C}}[p,q,\xi,\eta]$},

where

(221)

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{l}c(f,g)=-{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{p}}}\int_{0}^{p}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{\xi}}}dp+{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{p}}}\int_{0}^{p}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}dp+\xi{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi}}}\left((-1)^{p(f)}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{\eta}}}+{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\eta}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{\xi}}}\right)\Big|_{p=0,q=0}+\\
+{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta}}}\left(\int_{(0,q)}^{(p,q)}\left((-1)^{p(f)}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{p}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{\xi}}}-{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{p}}}\right)dp+\int_{(0,0)}^{(0,q)}\left((-1)^{p(f)}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{q}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{\xi}}}-{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\xi}}}{\frac{\partial{g}}{\partial{q}}}\right)\Big|_{p=0}dq\right).\end{array}

10.1.2.
{\mathfrak{h}}(2|2)
has more deformations than
{\mathfrak{po}}(2|2)
.

Note that the formula

[H_{f},H_{g}]_{new}=H_{\{f,g\}_{P.B.}}+\hbar(\lambda)\cdot H_{c(f,g)}

defines a deformation of
{\mathfrak{h}}(2|2)
(which is the main
deformation of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(2)
), but (and this agrees with an
unpublished preprint by Batalin and Tyutin) the formula

(222)

\{f,g\}_{new}=\{f,g\}_{P.B.}+\hbar(\lambda)\cdot c(f,g)

does not define a deformation of
{\mathfrak{po}}(2|2)
because the expression (
222
)
does not satisfy the Jacobi identity.

10.2.
Deformations of
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(n;n)

Clearly,
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}
is isomorphic to
\Pi(\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits})
. Therefore,
there is an embedding

(223)

{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(n;n)\subset\begin{cases}{\mathfrak{h}}:={\mathfrak{h}}(2^{2k-1}|2^{2k-1}),&\text{
for $n=2k$},\\
{\mathfrak{le}}:={\mathfrak{le}}(2^{2k}),&\text{ for $n=2k+1$.}\end{cases}

It is tempting to determine quantizations of
{\mathfrak{g}}
in addition to
those considered by Kotchetkov, as the composition of the embedding
(
223
) and subsequent quantization.

For
n=2
, when (
223
) is not just an embedding, but an
isomorphism, this idea certainly works and we get the following extra
quantization of the antibracket described in Theorem
2.12.1.  Theorem
:
we first deform the antibracket to the point
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}

along the main deformation, and then quantize it as the quotient of
the Poisson superalgebra. Let us show that this scheme fails to give new algebras for

n=2k>2
.

Theorem

(Deformations of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(n;n)
)
.

For
n=2k>2
, the image of

{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(n;n)
under the embedding (
223
) is rigid under the
quantization of the ambient.

Proof.

Consider the Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}:={\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(n;n)\subset{\mathfrak{m}}(n;n)
. Recall that, in this grading, the depth of
{\mathfrak{g}}
is
equal to 1, and

{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}=\Pi(\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{V\hskip-2.36806pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}),\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}={\mathfrak{vect}}(0|n).

Consider the case of embedding into
{\mathfrak{h}}
, see (
223
); the
case of embedding into
{\mathfrak{le}}
is similar. Denote the indeterminates
corresponding to
\Pi(\Lambda^{\mathbin{\hbox{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{\bf.}}}}\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits})\simeq\Pi(\oplus\Lambda^{i})
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{the ones corresponding to $i\leq k-2$ by $q=(q_{1},\dots,q_{N})$,}\\
\text{the ones corresponding to $i=k-1$ by $Q=(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{M})$,}\\
\text{the ones corresponding to $i=k$ by $x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{L})$,}\\
\text{the ones corresponding to $i=k+1$ by $P=(P_{1},\dots,P_{M})$,}\\
\text{the ones corresponding to $i>k+1$ by $p=(p_{1},\dots,p_{N})$}.\end{array}

The symplectic form determines the pairing (of the differentials) of

p
with
q
,
P
with
Q
and the
x
’s with themselves splitting
them into either two dual groups for
k=2l
(call them
y
and
z
),
or into three groups (two dual ones and one, consisting of one element
x_{0}
,
self-dual) for
k=2l+1
.

If all types of indeterminates are present, then
n\geq 4
.

Let
V=\mathop{\oplus}_{i\geq-1}\ V_{i}
be the projection of

{\mathfrak{g}}
to the space of vector fields generated by polynomials in
p,P,x,Q,q
. Of course,
V
must not be a subalgebra in
{\mathfrak{h}}
, but
it is invariant with respect to the partial derivatives
{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p}}},{\frac{\partial}{\partial{P}}},{\frac{\partial}{\partial{x}}},{\frac{\partial}{\partial{Q}}},{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q}}}
because

V_{-1}={\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}={\mathfrak{h}}_{-1}
.

Consider
V_{0}
. The
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
-action on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}
is such that

\Lambda^{s}
goes into
\mathop{\oplus}_{i\geq s-1}\ \Lambda^{i}
, so we deduce the following 1)–6):

1) The indeterminates
(p,P)
can go only into
(p,P,x)
, but
never into
(Q,q)
. In turn, this means that
V_{0}
does not contain
monomials of the form
q_{i}q_{j}
,
q_{i}Q_{j}
and
Q_{i}Q_{j}
.

Therefore, the subspace
V_{0}
contains only the monomials of degree

\leq 1
in
q,Q
.

2) The indeterminates
x
can go only into
(p,P)
, into
x
, and

Q
, but never into
q
. In turn, this means that
V_{0}
can contain
monomials of the form
x_{i}x_{j}
and
x_{i}Q_{j}
but not
x_{i}q_{j}
.

Consider a copy
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
, with the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading
given by

\deg\xi_{i}=\deg\tau=1,\ \ \deg u_{i}=0\text{~~for the remaining indeterminates $u_{i}$}.

Consider the bigrading of
{\mathfrak{g}}
given by

{\mathfrak{g}}:=\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i,j\geq-1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{i,j},\text{ where }{\mathfrak{g}}_{i,j}={\mathfrak{g}}_{i}\cap\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}.

The monomials of the form
x_{i}x_{j}
enter the decomposion of elements
from
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0,0}
, whereas the monomials of the form
x_{i}Q_{j}
enter
the decomposion of elements of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0,-1}
.

Does
V_{0}
contain degree-3 monomials in
x
? If yes,
they correspond to the elements of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1,t}
, where
k-1+t=0
,

i.e.
,
k=1-t
, and hence
k\leq 2
since
t\geq-1
.

Therefore, if
k>2
, and hence
n>4
, then the subspace
V_{0}
contains only the monomials of degree
\leq 2
in
x
.

3) Similarly, for
k>1
, and hence
n>2
, the subspace
V_{1}
does not
contain monomials of the form
x_{i}x_{j}Q_{s}
.

Therefore, if
n>4
, then the monomials belonging to
V
can be only of
degree
\leq 2
in
x,Q,q
, and monomials of degree 2 in
x,Q,q

should be of the form
f(p,P)x_{i}x_{j}
or
f(p,P)x_{i}Q_{j}
, and

\begin{array}[]{l}V\subset{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]x_{i})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]Q_{i})\bigoplus\\
(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]q_{i})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]x_{i}x_{j})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]x_{i}Q_{j}).\end{array}

Denote the summands above by
W
,
W_{x}
,
W_{Q}
,
W_{q}
,
W_{xx}
and

W_{xQ}
, respectively.

Consider the quantization of
{\mathfrak{h}}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}p_{i}\longmapsto p_{i},\quad P_{i}\longmapsto P_{i},\quad y_{i}\longmapsto y_{i},\\
q_{i}\longmapsto-(-1)^{p(p_{i})}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{p_{i}}}},\quad Q_{i}\longmapsto-(-1)^{p(P_{i})}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{P_{i}}}},\quad z_{i}\longmapsto-(-1)^{p(y_{i})}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{y_{i}}}}.\end{array}

We see that
W
goes into operators of multiplication by functions,
whereas
W_{x}
,
W_{Q}
and
W_{q}
turn into vector fields. Therefore, the
commutators of the elements of
W\oplus W_{x}\oplus W_{Q}\oplus W_{q}

do not change under the quantization.

Since the space
W\oplus W_{Q}\oplus W_{q}
does not depend on
x
,
the commutators of its elements with the elements of
W_{xx}\oplus W_{xQ}
do not change, either.

Thus, all deformations (“quantizations”) that change the
commutators concern only the brackets of
W_{xQ}
with elements of

W_{x}\oplus W_{xx}\oplus W_{xQ}
.

4) Since the monomials
x_{i}x_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0,0}
and
Q_{j}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1,k-2}
and since
\{P_{s}x_{i}x_{j},Q_{s}\}_{P.b.}=\pm x_{i}x_{j}
,
it follows that the monomials
P_{s}x_{i}x_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1,l}
, where

l+k-2=0
and
l\geq-1
. Hence,
k=2-l
and for
k>3
(
i.e.
, for

n>6
) the subspace
V_{1}
does not contain elements of the form
P_{s}x_{i}x_{j}
, and hence
W_{xx}
is of the form

\mathop{\oplus}_{i,j}\ {\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}x_{j}
.

5) Since the monomials
x_{i}Q_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0,-1}
and since
\{P_{s}x_{i}Q_{j},Q_{s}\}_{P.b.}=\pm x_{i}Q_{j}
, it follows that the monomials
P_{s}x_{i}Q_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1,l}
, where
l+k-2=-1
and
l\geq-1
.
Hence,
k=1-l
and for
k>2
(
i.e.
, for
n>4
) the subspace
V_{1}
does
not contain elements of the form
P_{s}x_{i}Q_{j}
, and hence
W_{xQ}

is of the form
\mathop{\oplus}_{i,j}\ {\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}Q_{j}
.

6) Since the monomials
x_{i}P_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0,1}
and since

\{P_{s}P_{i}x_{j},Q_{s}\}_{P.b.}=\pm P_{i}x_{j}
, it follows that the monomials

P_{s}P_{i}x_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1,l}
, where
l+k-2=1
and
l\geq-1
.
Hence,
k=3-l
and for
k>4
(
i.e.
, for
n>8
) the subspace
V_{1}
does
not contain elements of the form
P_{s}P_{i}x_{j}
, and hence
W_{x}

is of the form
\mathop{\oplus}_{i}\,\left({\mathbb{C}}[p]\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}_{j}\,{\mathbb{C}}[p]P_{j})x_{i}\right)
.

Thus, for
n>8
, the space
W_{x}\oplus W_{xx}\oplus W_{xQ}
has only
elements of degree
\leq 2
in
P,x,Q
:

\begin{array}[]{l}W_{x}\oplus W_{xx}\oplus W_{xQ}=\\
{\mathbb{C}}[p]\otimes\left((\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}x_{i})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{I,j}{\mathbb{C}}{\mathbb{P}}_{j}x_{i})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}x_{i}x_{j})\bigoplus(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i}{\mathbb{C}}x_{i}Q_{j})\right).\end{array}

The quantization preserves the brackets of embedded vector fields.

Let
n=8
. Then,
W_{xx}=\mathop{\oplus}_{i,j}\ {\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}x_{j}
and

W_{xQ}=\mathop{\oplus}_{i,j}\ {\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}Q_{j}
, so commutators of the
elements of these spaces do not change under quantization.

Consider brackets of elements of
W_{xQ}
and
W_{x}
. The effect of
quantization can appear only in brackets of order-2
differential operators with functions, but in the case under consideration
the coefficients of order-2 differential operators are constant
with respect to
x,P
, so

\begin{array}[]{l}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{j}\partial P_{k}},f(P)x_{j}\right]=(-1)^{p(x_{j})}{\frac{\partial{f(P)}}{\partial{P_{k}}}}x_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}+f(P)\partial_{P_{k}}+{\frac{\partial{f(P)}}{\partial{P_{k}}}}=\\
\widehat{\left\{x_{j}Q_{k},f(P)x_{j}\right\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{under quantization}}}{{=}}\\
\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits\left((-1)^{p(x_{j})}{\frac{\partial{f(P)}}{\partial{P_{k}}}}x_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}+f(P)\partial_{P_{k}}\right).\end{array}

Here, it is important that
W_{xQ}
does not depend on
P
,
i.e.
,
the coefficients of order-2 differential operators are constant with
respect to
x,P
and
the degree of functions is immaterial
, so
for
n=6
the brackets of elements of
W_{xQ}
and
W_{x}
do not change
under quantization, either.

Let now
n=6
. Then,

W_{xx}=\{\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i,j}{\mathbb{C}}[p,P]x_{i}x_{j}\mid\text{ the degree with respect to $P$ is $\leq 1\}$. }

Indeed, the elements
P_{s}x_{i}x_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1,-1}
and

Q_{t}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1,1}
, and since
\{P_{t}P_{s}x_{i}x_{j},Q_{t}\}_{P.b.}=\pm P_{s}x_{i}x_{j}
, it follows that the elements
P_{t}P_{s}x_{i}x_{j}
go to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2,l}
, where
l+1=-1
, which is a contradiction.

The elements of the form
{\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}x_{j}
and
{\mathbb{C}}[p]x_{i}x_{j}
lie in
\widetilde{V}_{-1}
. ∎

11.
The exceptional simple vectorial Lie superalgebra

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)

In this section, already used in
[
BGLLS
]
, we illustrate the algorithm presented in detail in

[
Shch
]
and rectify one formula from
[
CCK
]
. This algorithm
allows one to describe vectorial Lie superalgebras by means of
differential equations. In
[
Sh5
,
Sh14
]
the algorithm was used
to describe the exceptional simple vectorial Lie superalgebras of
depth 1.

The Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
has three realizations as a
transitive and primitive (
i.e.
, not preserving invariant foliations
on the space it is realized by means of vector fields) vectorial Lie
superalgebra. Speaking algebraically, the requirement to be a
transitive and primitive vectorial Lie superalgebra is formulated as
having a W-filtration, so
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
has three such
filtrations.

Two of these W-filtrations are of depth 2, and one is of depth 3.
In this section, we consider the latter case (the grading
K
),
i.e.
,
we explicitly solve the differential equations that single out our Lie
superalgebra. We thus explicitly obtain the expressions for the
elements of
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5;K)
. In particular, we verify and
rectify the formulas for the brackets given in
[
CCK
]
.

The Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
first appeared in
[
Sh3
]
. The
construction was performed in two steps:

1) Take
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}={\mathfrak{vect}}(0|3)
with the natural action
T^{1/2}
on the space

{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}=\Pi(\Lambda(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3})\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{1/2})

of semidensities with the inverted parity, see formula (
124
). The Cartan prolong of the pair
({\mathfrak{g}}_{-1},{\mathfrak{g}}_{0})_{*}
forms the Lie
superalgebra
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3)
.

2) Let us describe an embedding of the Lie superalgebra

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
we wish to define into
{\mathfrak{m}}(4)
. We work in terms of
generating functions and sometimes, by abuse of notation, write
f

instead of
M_{f}
.

The exceptional simple Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
is the
complete prolong in
{\mathfrak{m}}(4)
of the trivial central extension
of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3)
considered as the Lie subsuperalgebra

{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))\subset{\mathfrak{m}}(4)
, the
central element
\tau\in{\mathfrak{m}}
being added to the 0th component of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3)
.

Formally speaking,
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)
is defined. To explicitly describe
its elements, let us work a bit more. To avoid confusion, we assume
that the elements of
{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))
are generated by functions
of
\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{3},u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}
, whereas the elements of

{\mathfrak{m}}(4)
are generated by functions of
\xi_{i}
and
q_{i}
for
i=0,1,2,3
, and
\tau
.

Recapitulation from
[
Shch
]
. Let
{\mathfrak{n}}
be the nilpotent
Lie superalgebra; we wish realize
{\mathfrak{n}}
by vector fields,
i.e.
,
embed
{\mathfrak{n}}
into
{\mathfrak{vect}}(\dim{\mathfrak{n}})
so that the image of
{\mathfrak{n}}
is
spanned by all partial derivatives modulo the vector fields that vanish
at the origin. Such an embedding determines a nonstandard grading of
certain depth
d
on
{\mathfrak{vect}}(\dim{\mathfrak{n}})
. Denote
{\mathfrak{vect}}(\dim{\mathfrak{n}})

endowed with this nonstandard grading by
{\mathfrak{v}}=\mathop{\oplus}_{-d\leq k}^{\infty}{\mathfrak{v}}_{k}
. Let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
be the
image of
{\mathfrak{n}}
in
{\mathfrak{v}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
be spanned by the
left-invariant vector fields
X_{i}
on the supergroup
N
whose Lie
superalgebra is
{\mathfrak{n}}
. Let the
Y_{i}
be the right-invariant vector
fields on the supergroup
N
that also span
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
and such that

X_{i}(e)=Y_{i}(e),

where
e
is the unit of
N
. We have

M_{\xi_{i}}={\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{i}}}}+\xi_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\tau}}};\quad M_{q_{i}}=-{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{i}}}}+q_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\tau}}},

and the operators
Y
are (as follows from
[
Shch
]
) of the form:

Y_{q_{i}}={\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{i}}}}-\xi_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\tau}}};\quad Y_{\xi_{i}}={\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{i}}}}+q_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\tau}}}.

According to
[
Shch
]
, the equations for generating functions
should be expressed in terms of the operators
Y_{q_{i}}
and

\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{i}}:=Y_{\xi_{i}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{{P\hskip-1.29167ptt\hskip-0.21529pty\/}}}\nolimits
,
i.e.
,
\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{i}}(f)=(-1)^{p(f)}Y_{\xi_{i}}(f)
. We have

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{-}={\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))_{-}\simeq{\mathfrak{m}}(4)_{-}.

Here is the explicit correspondence between elements of degree
-1
:

(224)

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{-1}

{\mathfrak{m}}_{-1}(4)

\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\eta_{3}

\xi_{0}

\eta_{2}\eta_{3},\eta_{3}\eta_{1},\eta_{1}\eta_{2}

q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}

\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{3}

\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}

1

q_{0}

The 0th component of
{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))
is isomorphic to

{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|3)
and its action on the
(-1)
st component is the same
as on the space of semidensities. It is easy to see that the
supertrace of any such operator is equal to
0
. Thus, we get
embeddings
({\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3)))_{0}\subset{\mathfrak{spe}}(4)
and

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}\subset{\mathfrak{b}}_{1,0}(4)
.

Comparing the actions of various elements of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3)_{0}\simeq{\mathfrak{vect}}(0|3)
and
{\mathfrak{spe}}(4)\subset{\mathfrak{m}}(4)_{0}
we get the following
identifications (here
(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
, the group of even permutations):

\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{i}\longleftrightarrow-q_{0}q_{i}+\xi_{j}\xi_{k},\\
\eta_{i}\partial_{i}\longleftrightarrow\frac{1}{2}(-q_{0}\xi_{0}-q_{i}\xi_{i}+q_{j}\xi_{j}+q_{k}\xi_{k}),\\
\eta_{i}\partial_{j}\longleftrightarrow-q_{j}\xi_{i}\text{~~for $i\neq j$},\\
\eta_{j}\eta_{k}\partial_{i}\longleftrightarrow-\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2},\\
\end{array}\begin{array}[]{l}\eta_{i}(\eta_{j}\partial_{j}-\eta_{k}\partial_{k})\longleftrightarrow-\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-q_{j}q_{k},\\
\eta_{i}(\mathop{\sum}\limits_{j}\eta_{j}\partial_{j})\longleftrightarrow\xi_{0}\xi_{i},\\
\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\eta_{3}\partial_{i}\longleftrightarrow-\frac{1}{2}\xi_{0}q_{i}.\end{array}\end{array}

Since
\dim{\mathfrak{m}}(4)_{0}=17|16
and
\dim{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(4|5)_{0}=13|12
, we need

4|4
equations to single out
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}
in
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}
.

Note that we have a natural
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading of
{\mathfrak{m}}_{-1}
induced
by the identification
{\mathfrak{m}}_{-1}\simeq\Pi(\Lambda(\eta))
; this
grading induces a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading on
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}
as well. Let
\deg
be
the degree of a given operator with respect to this
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading;
let
N
be the number of even
|
odd equations; let
i
and
j
run
over the set
\{1,2,3\}
; let the triple
(ijk)
be a permutation of

(123)
. We get

(225)

\deg

{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}

N

equations

-3

q_{0}^{2}

—

0|1

Y_{q_{0}}^{2}(f)=0

-2

q_{0}\xi_{i},

—

3|0

Y_{q_{0}}\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{i}}(f)=0

-1

q_{0}q_{i},\;\xi_{i}\xi_{j}

-q_{0}q_{i}+\xi_{j}\xi_{k}

0|3

\left(Y_{q_{0}}Y_{q_{i}}+\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{j}}\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{k}}\right)(f)=0

0

q_{i}\xi_{j}

q_{i}\xi_{j},\;i\neq j

1|0

q_{0}\xi_{0}

-q_{0}\xi_{0}-q_{i}\xi_{i}+q_{j}\xi_{j}+q_{k}\xi_{k}

\mathop{\sum}\limits_{0\leq i\leq 3}\left(Y_{q_{i}}\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{i}}+\widehat{Y}_{\xi_{i}}Y_{q_{i}}\right)(f)=0

1

\xi_{i}\xi_{0},q_{i}q_{j}

\xi_{i}\xi_{0},q_{i}q_{j}

0

2

\xi_{0}q_{i}

\xi_{0}q_{i}

0

3

—

—

0

The equations are in such a form that we can discard the hats over
the operators
Y_{\xi_{i}}
. After that, we see that
the functions that generate elements of the subalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}
are
solutions of the following system of
4|4
equations (here
i=1,2,3

and
(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
):

\begin{array}[]{ll}Y_{q_{0}}^{2}(f)=0,&Y_{q_{0}}Y_{\xi_{i}}(f)=0,\\
\left(Y_{q_{0}}Y_{q_{i}}-Y_{\xi_{j}}Y_{\xi_{k}}\right)(f)=0,&\mathop{\sum}\limits_{0\leq i\leq 3}\left(Y_{q_{i}}Y_{\xi_{i}}+Y_{\xi_{i}}Y_{q_{i}}\right)(f)=0,\end{array}

or, more explicitly,

(226)

\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}^{2}}-2\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{0}}}}{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\tau}}}=0,

(227)

\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}\partial\xi_{i}}+\left(q_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{0}}}}+\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{i}}}}\right){\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\tau}}}=0,

(228)

\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}\partial q_{i}}-\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial\xi_{j}\partial\xi_{k}}-\left(\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{i}}}}+\xi_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{0}}}}-q_{j}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{k}}}}+q_{k}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{j}}}}\right){\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\tau}}}=0,

(229)

\Delta f+E{\frac{\partial{f}}{\partial{\tau}}}=0.

The last equation means precisely that
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}
lies inside of

{\mathfrak{b}}_{1,0}(4)
.

Set
q=(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
,
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})
. Each of the
equations (
226
)–(
229
) is of the form

D(f)=(D_{0}+D_{1}\partial_{\tau})(f)=0,

where the operators
D_{0}
and
D_{1}
do not depend on
\tau
. On the
space of functions of the form
f=f_{0}+\tau f_{1}
, where
f_{0},f_{1}\in{\mathbb{C}}[q,q_{0},\xi,\xi_{0}]
, each of these equations is equivalent to the
system

\begin{cases}D_{0}f_{1}=0,\\
D_{0}f_{0}+D_{1}f_{1}=0.\end{cases}

Therefore, the solutions of the system (
226
)–(
229
)
independent of
\tau
(they generate the image of

{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))
in
{\mathfrak{m}}(4)
), as well as the coefficients of
\tau

in all the solutions of the system (
226
)–(
229
), are
solutions of the following system (here
i=1,2,3
and
(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
):

(230)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}^{2}}=0,\qquad\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}\partial\xi_{i}}=0,\\
\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial q_{0}\partial q_{i}}-\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial\xi_{j}\partial\xi_{k}}=0,\qquad\Delta f=0.\end{array}

The first two groups of equations in (
230
) imply that

f=\varphi(q,\xi,\xi_{0})+q_{0}\varphi_{1}(q,\xi_{0}).

Differentiating the equations of the third group in
(
230
) with respect to
\xi_{i}
, and taking into account the
second group we get

\frac{\partial^{3}f}{\partial\xi_{1}\partial\xi_{2}\partial\xi_{3}}=0.

Moreover, the third group of equations (
230
) implies that

\frac{\partial\varphi_{1}}{\partial q_{i}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial\xi_{j}\partial\xi_{k}},

i.e.
,

(231)

f=\varphi_{0}(q,\xi,\xi_{0})-\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi_{1})+q_{0}\varphi_{1}(q,\xi_{0}),\quad\text{where $\deg_{\xi}\varphi_{0}\leq 1$.}

Conversely, any function of the form (
231
) such that

\Delta f=\Delta\varphi_{0}+{\frac{\partial{\varphi_{1}}}{\partial{\xi_{0}}}}=0
is a solution of
eq. (
230
), and hence of the system
(
226
)–(
229
).

Explicit solutions are:

(232)

f(q),\quad f(q)\xi_{0},

(233)

F_{f}=f-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\Delta f),\;\text{ where }\;f=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i},

(234)

\xi_{0}\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i},\;\text{ where }\;\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}=0,

(235)

q_{0}f(q)-\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}f).

Now let us find the solutions for which the coefficient of
\tau

is of the form (
232
)–(
235
). Set

(236)

\Phi=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}q_{i}\xi_{i}.

The solutions of (
232
) are obviously given by

\xi_{0}\left(\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{3}(\tau-\Phi)\Delta f\right),\text{ where }f=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i},

and

kq_{0}\xi_{0}f(q)-(k+1)\xi_{0}\Delta(f\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3})-\tau f,\text{
where }k=\deg f.

Combining this last solution with
F_{f}
from (
233
) we get a
solution of the form

\widetilde{F}_{f}=f+q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\frac{1}{2}(\tau-\Phi-q_{0}\xi_{0})\Delta f,\text{ where }f=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i}.

The functions
F_{f}
and
\widetilde{F}_{f}
determine two embeddings of

{\mathfrak{vect}}(3|0)
into
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}
. From general considerations the
embedded algebras should be obtained from one another by regradings
of the ambient algebra.

Now, let us find solutions with coefficient of
\tau
of the form
(
233
) with
\Delta f=0
. Then,
f
can be represented as
f=\Delta\widetilde{f}
for a function
\widetilde{f}
of the
form

\widetilde{f}=\sum f^{i}(q)\xi_{j}\xi_{k},\qquad\text{where $(i,j,k)\in A_{3}$}.

Take

\widehat{f}=q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta\widetilde{f}+\tau\Delta\widetilde{f}.

Then,
\widehat{f}
satisfies the system (
226
)–(
227
), and
(
228
) implies:

\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\partial^{2}\widehat{f}}{\partial q_{0}\partial q_{i}}-\frac{\partial^{2}\widehat{f}}{\partial\xi_{j}\partial\xi_{k}}-\left(\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{i}}}}+\xi_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{0}}}}-q_{j}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{k}}}}+q_{k}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{j}}}}\right){\frac{\partial{\widehat{f}}}{\partial{\tau}}}=\\
\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial{\Delta\widetilde{f}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}-\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial{\Delta\widetilde{f}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}+q_{j}{\frac{\partial{\Delta\widetilde{f}}}{\partial{\xi_{k}}}}-q_{k}{\frac{\partial{\Delta\widetilde{f}}}{\partial{\xi_{j}}}}=\\
q_{j}\left({\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{j}}}}-{\frac{\partial{f^{j}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}\right)-q_{k}\left({\frac{\partial{f^{k}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}-{\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{k}}}}\right)=\\
q_{i}{\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}+q_{j}{\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{j}}}}+q_{k}{\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{k}}}}-q_{i}{\frac{\partial{f^{i}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}-q_{j}{\frac{\partial{f^{j}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}-q_{k}{\frac{\partial{f^{k}}}{\partial{q_{i}}}}=\\
E(f^{i})-\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{i}}(q_{i}f^{i}+q_{j}f^{j}+q_{k}f^{k})+f^{i}=\\
E(f^{i})-{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{i}}}}\left(\left(\int_{\xi}\Phi\right)\cdot\widetilde{f}\right)+f^{i}.\end{array}

Therefore, it is easy to fix up
\widehat{f}
to get a solution:

F=\widetilde{f}-E(\widetilde{f})+q_{0}\left(\int_{\xi}\Phi\widetilde{f}\right)+q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta\widetilde{f}+\tau\Delta\widetilde{f}.

It is subject to a direct verification that
F
satisfies also
(
229
).

We similarly see that, starting with
\widehat{f}=\tau\xi_{0}\Delta f
, where
f=\sum f^{i}(q)\xi_{j}\xi_{k}
, we arrive at
the function

F_{1}=-q_{0}\xi_{0}\left(\int_{\xi}\Phi f\right)+\xi_{0}(E-1)(f)+\tau\xi_{0}\Delta f,

which satisfies the system (
226
)–(
228
). However,

\Delta F_{1}+E{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\tau}}}=-\int_{\xi}(\Phi f).

Adding to
F_{1}
the function
\varphi=\sum\varphi_{i}\xi_{i}
such that
\Delta\varphi=\int_{(\xi)}(\Phi f)
we get a solution of the system
(
226
)–(
229
) which, by using the obtained solutions, can be simplified to

F=\xi_{0}\left(-\Delta\bigg(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\left(\int_{\xi}\Phi f\right)\bigg)+(E-1)(f)-\tau\Delta f\right).

So we have obtained
a solution in which the coefficient of
\tau
is of the form (
234
).

Similarly, if we want to get a solution in which the coefficient of
\tau

is of the form (
235
),
i.e.
, equal to

q_{0}\varphi(q)-\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi),

we take

F_{1}=q_{0}^{2}\xi_{0}\varphi+\tau\left(q_{0}\varphi(q)-\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi)\right)

to satisfy (
226
), then pass to

F_{2}=F_{1}-q_{0}\Phi\varphi-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi)

to satisfy (
227
), and, finally, we pass to

F=q_{0}(q_{0}\xi_{0}-\Phi)\varphi-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi)+\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}(E+2)\varphi+\tau\left(q_{0}\varphi(q)-\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\varphi)\right)

to satisfy (
228
). Now, one can verify that condition (
229
) is
satisfied.

Finally, let us obtain a solution in which the coefficient of
\tau
is an
arbitrary function of the form (
235
). We start with the
function

F_{0}=\tau(f-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\Delta f)),\text{ where }f=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(q)\xi_{i}.

This
F_{0}
satisfies (
226
), and

\left(q_{i}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{q_{0}}}}+\xi_{0}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\xi_{i}}}}\right){\frac{\partial{F_{0}}}{\partial{\tau}}}=-q_{i}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\xi_{0}f_{i}.

To satisfy (
227
) we pass to

F_{1}=q_{0}\xi_{0}(f-\Phi\Delta f)+F_{0}=q_{0}\xi_{0}(f-\Phi\Delta f)+\tau(f-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\Delta f)).

Next, to satisfy (
228
), we pass to

F_{2}=\xi_{0}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}(E+2)(\Delta f)-\Phi f+F_{1},

and check that (
229
) is also satisfied. Finally, we get the
solution

F=F_{2}=\xi_{0}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}(E+2)(\Delta f)-\Phi f+q_{0}\xi_{0}(f-\Phi\Delta f)+\tau(f-q_{0}\xi_{0}\Delta f+\xi_{0}\Delta(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\Delta f)).

11.1.
The W-gradings of
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)

We will again assume that the
elements of the Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))
that we have
started with in order to define the Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)

are given by generating functions in
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3},p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
, whereas the basis element of the added center is
z
. The
regrading of
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
is obtained by setting

\deg p_{i}=2,\quad\deg\zeta_{i}=1\text{ for }i=1,2,3,\quad\deg_{Lie}(f)=\deg(f)-3.

This regrading is naturally extended to the whole Lie superalgebra

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
and is of depth 3. The component
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}
is spanned
by
1\in{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{1/2}(3;3))
and the added center
z
.

In this realization, the Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
is the
complete prolong of its negative part
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}={\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{-2}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}
, where

{\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}=\mathop{\mathrm{{Span}}}\nolimits(1,z),\ {\mathfrak{g}}_{-2}=\mathop{\mathrm{{Span}}}\nolimits(\zeta_{i}\mid i=1,2,3),\;{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{{Span}}}\nolimits(p_{i},\zeta_{j}\zeta_{k}\mid i,j,k=1,2,3),

and
therefore

\dim{\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}=0|2,\quad\dim{\mathfrak{g}}_{-2}=3|0,\quad\dim{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}=0|6.

The non-zero brackets are as follows (here
i=1,2,3
,
(i,j,k)\in A_{3})
:

\{p_{i},\zeta_{i}\}=-1,\;\;\;\{\zeta_{i}\zeta_{j},\zeta_{k}\}=-z,\;\;\;\{p_{i},\zeta_{i}\zeta_{j}\}=-\zeta_{j},\;\;\;\{p_{i},\zeta_{k}\zeta_{i}\}=\zeta_{k}.

In particular, the center of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
coincides with
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}
.

We use this Lie superalgebra to demonstrate how to work with
generating functions.

First of all, we embed
{\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
into the Lie superalgebra

{\mathfrak{v}}={\mathfrak{vect}}(3|8)={\mathfrak{der}}~\,{\mathbb{C}}[u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\mid\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{3},\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3},\chi_{1},\chi_{2}]

considered with the grading (for any
i,j
)

\deg\eta_{i}=\deg\zeta_{i}=1,\quad\deg u_{i}=2,\quad\deg\chi_{j}=3.

In accordance with
[
Shch
]
, we find in
{\mathfrak{v}}_{-}
two mutually
commuting families of elements:
X
-vectors and
Y
-vectors. The
table of correspondences reads

(237)

k

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{-k}

X

Y

-1

q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}

X_{\eta_{i}}

Y_{\eta_{i}}

\xi_{2}\xi_{3},\xi_{3}\xi_{1},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}

X_{\zeta_{i}}

Y_{\zeta_{i}}

-2

\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}

X_{u_{i}}

Y_{u_{i}}

-3

1,\widehat{1}

X_{\chi_{j}}

Y_{\chi_{j}}

The non-zero commutation relations for the
X
-vectors, where

(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
, are

\begin{array}[]{ll}{}[X_{\eta_{i}},X_{u_{i}}]=-X_{\chi_{1}},&[X_{\zeta_{i}},X_{u_{i}}]=-X_{\chi_{2}},\\
{}[X_{\eta_{i}},X_{\zeta_{k}}]=-X_{u_{j}},&[X_{\eta_{i}},X_{\zeta_{j}}]=X_{u_{k}}.\end{array}

The non-zero commutation relations for the
Y
-vectors correspond to
the negative of the above structure constants:

\begin{array}[]{ll}{}[Y_{\eta_{i}},Y_{u_{i}}]=Y_{\chi_{1}},&[Y_{\zeta_{i}},Y_{u_{i}}]=Y_{\chi_{2}},\\
{}[Y_{\eta_{i}},Y_{\zeta_{k}}]=Y_{u_{j}},&[Y_{\eta_{i}},Y_{\zeta_{j}}]=-Y_{u_{k}}.\end{array}

Let us represent an arbitrary vector field
X\in{\mathfrak{vect}}(3|8)
in the
form

(238)

X=F_{1}Y_{\chi_{1}}+F_{2}Y_{\chi_{2}}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(f_{\xi_{i}}Y_{\xi_{i}}+f_{\eta_{i}}Y_{\eta_{i}}+f_{u_{i}}Y_{u_{i}}\right).

As it was shown in
[
Shch
]
, any
X\in{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
is completely
determined by a pair of functions
F_{1},F_{2}
via the equations, where

i=1,2,3
and
(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
,

(239)

Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{1})=0,\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{1})=-(-1)^{p(f_{u_{i}})}f_{u_{i}}=Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2}),\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{2})=0,

(240)

Y_{\xi_{i}}(f_{u_{i}})=Y_{\eta_{i}}(f_{u_{i}})=0,

(241)

Y_{\xi_{i}}(f_{u_{j}})=(-1)^{p(f_{\eta_{k}})}f_{\eta_{k}},\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(f_{u_{j}})=-(-1)^{p(f_{\xi_{k}})}f_{\xi_{k}},

(242)

Y_{\xi_{i}}(f_{u_{k}})=-(-1)^{p(f_{\eta_{j}})}f_{\eta_{j}},\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(f_{u_{k}})=(-1)^{p(f_{\xi_{j}})}f_{\xi_{j}}.

Therefore, the functions
F_{1},F_{2}
must satisfy the following three
groups of equations:

(243)

Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{1})=0,\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{1})=Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2}),\quad Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{2})=0~~~\text{for $i=1,2,3$}.

The relations (
239
), (
241
) determine the remaining
coordinates, while the relations (
240
), (
242
) follow
from (
239
), (
241
) and the commutation relations that
Y

obey. Indeed,

Y_{\xi_{i}}(f_{u_{i}})=-(-1)^{p(f_{u_{i}})}Y_{\xi_{i}}Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2})=0

and

Y_{\xi_{i}}(f_{u_{j}})=-(-1)^{p(f_{u_{j}})}Y_{\xi_{i}}Y_{\xi_{j}}(F_{2})=(-1)^{p(f_{u_{i}})}Y_{\xi_{j}}Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2})=-Y_{\xi_{j}}(f_{u_{i}}),

since
p(f_{u_{j}})=p(f_{u_{i}})=p(X)
. Moreover,

f_{\xi_{k}}=-(-1)^{p(f_{\xi_{k}})}Y_{\eta_{i}}(f_{u_{j}})=-Y_{\eta_{i}}Y_{\xi_{j}}(F_{2})=Y_{\xi_{j}}Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{2})+Y_{u_{k}}(F_{2})=Y_{u_{k}}(F_{2}).

We similarly get the expressions for the remaining coordinates:

f_{\eta_{k}}=Y_{u_{k}}(F_{1}).

Therefore, an arbitrary element
X\in{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
is of the form

(244)

X=X^{F}=F_{1}Y_{\chi_{1}}+F_{2}Y_{\chi_{2}}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(Y_{u_{i}}(F_{2})Y_{\xi_{i}}+Y_{u_{i}}(F_{1})Y_{\eta_{i}}-(-1)^{p(X)}Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2})Y_{u_{i}}\right),

where the pair of functions
F=\{F_{1},F_{2}\}
satisfies the system of
equations (
243
).

We select the
Y
-vectors so that the equations (
243
) that the
functions
F_{1},F_{2}
should satisfy are as simple as possible. For
example, take the following
Y
-vectors, where
i=1,2,3
,
s=1,2
, and

(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}Y_{\eta_{i}}=\partial_{\eta_{i}}+\xi_{k}\partial_{u_{j}}-\xi_{j}\partial_{u_{k}}+(\xi_{k}\eta_{j}-\xi_{j}\eta_{k})\partial_{\chi_{1}}-\xi_{j}\xi_{k}\partial_{\chi_{2}},&Y_{\xi_{i}}=\partial_{\xi_{i}},\\
Y_{u_{i}}=\partial_{u_{i}}+\eta_{i}\partial_{\chi_{1}}+\xi_{i}\partial_{\chi_{2}},&Y_{\chi_{s}}=\partial_{\chi_{s}}.\end{array}

Then, the
X
-vectors are of the form

(245)

\begin{array}[]{ll}X_{\xi_{i}}=\partial_{\xi_{i}}-\eta_{j}\partial_{u_{k}}+\eta_{k}\partial_{u_{j}}-\eta_{j}\eta_{k}\partial_{\chi_{1}}+u_{i}\partial_{\chi_{2}},&X_{\eta_{i}}=\partial_{\eta_{i}}+u_{i}\partial_{\chi_{1}},\\
X_{\chi_{1}}=\partial_{\chi_{1}},\ \ \ X_{\chi_{2}}=\partial_{\chi_{2}}&X_{u_{i}}=\partial_{u_{i}}.\end{array}

The Lie superalgebra
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
consists of the vector fields that
preserve the distribution determined by the following equations
for the vector field
D
of the form (
238
):

(246)

f_{u_{1}}=f_{u_{2}}=f_{u_{3}}=F_{1}=F_{2}=0.

Let us express the coordinates
f
of the field
D
in the
Y
-basis
in terms of the standard coordinates in the basis of partial
derivatives:

D=g_{\chi_{1}}\partial_{\chi_{1}}+g_{\chi_{2}}\partial_{\chi_{1}}+\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3}\ \left(g_{\xi_{i}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}+g_{\eta_{i}}\partial_{\eta_{i}}+g_{u_{i}}\partial_{u_{i}}\right).

We get

\begin{array}[]{l}f_{u_{i}}=g_{u_{i}}+g_{\eta_{j}}\xi_{k}-g_{\eta_{k}}\xi_{j},\\
F_{1}=g_{\chi_{1}}-\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3}\ g_{u_{i}}\eta_{i},\\
F_{2}=g_{\chi_{2}}-\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3}\ g_{u_{i}}\xi_{i}-\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3,\ (i,j,k)\in A_{3}}g_{\eta_{i}}\xi_{j}\xi_{k}.\end{array}

Therefore, in the standard coordinates, the
distribution (
246
) is of the form (
i=1,2,3
):

(247)

\begin{array}[]{l}g_{u_{i}}+g_{\eta_{j}}\xi_{k}-g_{\eta_{k}}\xi_{j}=0,\\
g_{\chi_{1}}-\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3}g_{u_{i}}\ \eta_{i}=0,\\
g_{\chi_{2}}-\mathop{\sum}_{1\leq i\leq 3}g_{u_{i}}\xi_{i}-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3,\ (i,j,k)\in A_{3}}g_{\eta_{i}}\xi_{j}\xi_{k}=0.\end{array}

Using the first two relations we rewrite the third one
as

g_{\chi_{2}}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3,\ (i,j,k)\in A_{3}}g_{\eta_{i}}\xi_{j}\xi_{k}=0.

Assuming that the pairing between vectors and covectors is given by the formula

\langle f\partial_{\xi},gd\xi\rangle=(-1)^{p(g)}fg,

we see that the
distribution is specified by the Pfaff
equations given by the 1-forms

(248)

\begin{array}[]{l}du_{i}+\xi_{j}d\eta_{k}-\xi_{k}d\eta_{j},\\
d\chi_{1}-\mathop{\sum}\eta_{i}du_{i},\\
d\chi_{2}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3,\ (i,j,k)\in A_{3}}\xi_{j}\xi_{k}d\eta_{i}.\end{array}

Let us now solve the system (
243
).

Since
Y_{\xi_{i}}=\partial_{\xi_{i}}
, the equality
Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{1})=0

implies that
F_{1}=F_{1}(u,\eta,\chi)
. Further, the equality

Y_{\xi_{i}}(F_{2})=Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{1})=0

takes the form

{\frac{\partial{F_{2}}}{\partial{\xi_{i}}}}={\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\eta_{i}}}}+\left(\xi_{k}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}-\xi_{j}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}\right)+\left(\xi_{k}\eta_{j}-\xi_{j}\eta_{k}\right){\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}-\xi_{j}\xi_{k}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}},

whence (since
F_{1}
does not depend on
\xi
)

(249)

F_{2}=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\xi_{i}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\eta_{i}}}}-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3,~~(i,j,k)\in A_{3}}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\left({\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}+\eta_{k}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}\right)-\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}+\alpha_{2},

where
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{2}(u,\eta,\chi)
,
i.e.
, does not depend on

\xi
.

Let us expand the last group of equations

Y_{\eta_{i}}(F_{2})=0

in power series in
\xi
. Observe that the coefficient of

\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}
vanishes automatically.

The terms of degree-
0
in
\xi
are of the form

{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\eta_{i}}}}=0\Longrightarrow\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{2}(u,\chi).

The terms of degree-
1
in
\xi
are of the form

\xi_{k}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}-\xi_{j}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}+\left(\xi_{k}\eta_{j}-\xi_{j}\eta_{k}\right){\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}=\xi_{j}\frac{\partial^{2}F_{1}}{\partial\eta_{i}\partial\eta_{j}}+\xi_{k}\frac{\partial^{2}F_{1}}{\partial\eta_{i}\partial\eta_{k}},

implying that

(250)

F_{1}=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3,\ (i,j,k)\in A_{3}}\eta_{i}\eta_{j}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}+\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\eta_{3}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+\alpha_{1}(u,\chi)+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}f_{i}(u,\chi)\eta_{i}.

Therefore, the generating functions
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are completely
determined by the 5 functions

\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}
which
depend only on
u,\chi
.

The terms of degree-
2
in
\xi
lead to the equation (the
coefficient of
\xi_{j}\xi_{k}
):

(251)

\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq s\leq 3}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}F_{1}}{\partial u_{s}\partial\eta_{s}}-\eta_{s}{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\eta_{s}}}}{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}\right)+{\frac{\partial{F_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}=0.

Consider this equation by degrees in
\eta
. The degree
0
yields
the equation

(252)

\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}(-1)^{p(f_{i})}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}+{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}=0.

In degrees
1,2,3
in
\eta
the equation (
251
) is
automatically satisfied.

Let us express eq. (
252
) in the following more transparent form. Denote

\begin{array}[]{l}f_{i}=f_{i}^{0}+f_{i}^{1}\chi_{1}+f_{i}^{2}\chi_{2}+f_{i}^{12}\chi_{1}\chi_{2},\\
\alpha_{s}=\alpha_{s}^{0}+\alpha_{s}^{1}\chi_{1}+\alpha_{s}^{2}\chi_{2}+\alpha_{s}^{12}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}.\end{array}

Then, equation (
252
) is equivalent to the system

(253)

\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}^{12}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}=0,\quad\alpha_{1}^{12}-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}^{2}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}=0,\quad\alpha_{2}^{12}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}^{1}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}=0,\quad\alpha_{1}^{1}+\alpha_{2}^{2}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}^{0}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}=0.

To describe the commutation relations in
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)
more
explicitly, let us express the vector field (
244
) as

(254)

X^{F}=x^{F}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(f_{\xi_{i}}Y_{\xi_{i}}+f_{\eta_{i}}Y_{\eta_{i}}-(-1)^{p(X)}f_{u_{i}}Y_{u_{i}}\right),\text{~where $x^{F}=F_{1}\partial_{\chi_{1}}+F_{2}\partial_{\chi_{2}}$,}

and observe that, in view of relations (
239
) and (
241
), we have

[X^{F},X^{G}]=X^{H},

where

(255)

\begin{array}[]{l}H_{1}=[x^{F},x^{G}]_{1}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(f_{u_{i}}g_{\eta_{i}}-(-1)^{p(X^{G})}f_{\eta_{i}}g_{u_{i}}\right),\\
H_{2}=[x^{F},x^{G}]_{2}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(f_{u_{i}}g_{\xi_{i}}-(-1)^{p(X^{G})}f_{\xi_{i}}g_{u_{i}}\right).\end{array}

Observe that it suffices to compute only the
defining

components of
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
,
i.e.
, when the pair
F
is determined
by the set
\alpha_{s}
, where
s=1,2
, and
f_{i}
, where
i=1,2,3
,
the pair
G
is determined by the set
\beta_{s}
, where
s=1,2
, and

g_{i}
, where
i=1,2,3
, and the pair
H
is determined by the set

\gamma_{s}
, where
s=1,2
, and
h_{i}
, where
i=1,2,3
. Then, with
i=1,2,3
, and
(i,j,k)\in A_{3}
, we get

(256)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\gamma_{1}&=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(-f_{i}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{1}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}+(-1)^{p(X^{G})}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}g_{i}\right)+\\
&+\left(\alpha_{1}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+\alpha_{2}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}\right)-(-1)^{p(X^{F})p(X^{G})}\left(\beta_{1}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+\beta_{2}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}\right);\end{array}

(257)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\gamma_{2}&=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(-f_{i}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{2}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}+(-1)^{p(X^{G})}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}g_{i}\right)+\\
&+\left(\alpha_{1}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+\alpha_{2}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}\right)-(-1)^{p(X^{F})p(X^{G})}\left(\beta_{1}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{1}}}}+\beta_{2}{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{\chi_{2}}}}\right);\end{array}

(258)

\begin{array}[]{ll}h_{i}&=-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq r\leq 3}f_{r}{\frac{\partial{g_{i}}}{\partial{u_{r}}}}+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{1\leq r\leq 3}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{r}}}}g_{r}-\\
&(-1)^{p(X^{G})}\left({\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}-{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{1}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}-{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{2}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}+{\frac{\partial{\alpha_{1}}}{\partial{u_{k}}}}{\frac{\partial{\beta_{2}}}{\partial{u_{j}}}}\right)+\\
&+\mathop{\sum}\limits_{s=1,2}\alpha_{s}{\frac{\partial{g_{i}}}{\partial{\chi_{s}}}}-(-1)^{p(X^{F})p(X^{G})}\mathop{\sum}\limits_{s=1,2}\beta_{s}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{\chi_{s}}}}.\end{array}

In what follows, we identify the vector field
X^{F}
with the
collection

\{\alpha_{s},f_{i}\mid s=1,2,\quad i=1,2,3\}.

The bracket of vector fields corresponds to the bracket of such
collections given by eqs. (
256
)–(
258
).

Consider now the even part
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{0}}
of our algebra. Since

p(F_{1})=p(F_{2})={\bar{1}}
, it follows that
p(\alpha_{s})={\bar{1}}
,

p(f_{i})={\bar{0}}
. The component
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{0}}
admits a decomposition into three
subspaces described below

{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{0}}=V_{1}\oplus V_{2}\oplus V_{3}.

The subspace
V_{1}
is determined by the collection

\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=0,\;\;f_{i}=f_{i}(u)\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\mid\mathop{\sum}{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}=0\}.

Eqs. (
256
)–(
258
) imply that
the vector fields generated by such functions form a commutative
ideal in
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{0}}
; we will identify this ideal with

d\Omega^{1}(3)
via the mapping

\{0,0,f_{i}\mid i=1,2,3\}\longmapsto-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{i}f_{i}du_{j}du_{k}.

Next, the subspace
V_{2}
is determined by the collection
\{f_{i}=0\mid i=1,2,3\}
. We will identify this space with
\Omega^{0}(3)\otimes{\mathfrak{sl}}(2)
via the mapping

\{\alpha(u)(a\chi_{1}+b\chi_{2}),\alpha(u)(c\chi_{1}-a\chi_{2}),0,0,0\}\longmapsto\alpha(u)\otimes\begin{pmatrix}a&c\\
b&d\end{pmatrix}.

Eqs. (
256
)–(
258
) imply that the elements of the subspaces
V_{1}
and

V_{2}
commute, whereas the bracket of two
collections from
V_{2}
is, in our notation, of the form

[f\otimes A,g\otimes B]=fg\otimes[A,B]+df\wedge dg\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\nolimits AB.

Finally, for
V_{3}
we have the following three natural possibilities: we take
f_{i}=f_{i}(u)
for any
i=1,2,3
, while for the

\alpha
we make the following choices:

(a):

\alpha_{1}=-\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}=0
,

(b):

\alpha_{1}=0
,
\alpha_{2}=-\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{2}
,

(c):

\alpha_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{1}
,

\alpha_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{2}
.

In the cases (a) and (b), we get two embeddings:
{\mathfrak{vect}}(3)\subset{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{0}}
.

The case (c) is, however, more convenient to simplify the
brackets. Thus, we identify
V_{3}
with
{\mathfrak{vect}}(3)
via

\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{1},\ -\frac{1}{2}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{2},\ f_{1}(u),\ f_{2}(u),\ f_{3}(u)\right\}\longmapsto D_{f}=-\mathop{\sum}\limits_{i}f_{i}(u)\partial_{u_{i}}.

The action of
D_{f}
on the subspaces
V_{1}
(as on the space of

2
-forms) and
V_{2}
(as on the space of vector-valued functions

{\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathfrak{sl}}(2)
) is natural. The bracket of the operators
D_{f}

is, however, manifestly different from the usual bracket thanks to the presence of an
extra term:

[D_{f},D_{g}]=D_{f}D_{g}-D_{g}D_{f}-\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{1}{2}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{1}{2}$}}d(\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D_{f})\wedge d(\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D_{f}).

Consider now the odd part
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}(3|8)_{\bar{1}}
. We have

p(F_{1})=p(F_{2})={\bar{0}}
, and hence

p(\alpha_{s})={\bar{0}},\;\;p(f_{i})={\bar{1}}.

Let
V_{4}
consist of the collections with
f_{i}=0
. We identify
V_{4}

with
\Omega^{0}(3)\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
via

\{\alpha(u)w_{1},\ \alpha(u)w_{2},0,0,0\}\longmapsto\alpha(u)\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}w_{2}\\
-w_{1}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
V_{5}
consist of the collections of the form

(259)

\begin{array}[]{l}f_{i}=f_{i}(u)(v_{1}\chi_{1}+v_{2}\chi_{2})~~\text{for $i=1,2,3$},\quad\alpha_{1}=v_{2}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2},\quad\alpha_{2}=-v_{1}\sum{\frac{\partial{f_{i}}}{\partial{u_{i}}}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}.\end{array}

We identify
V_{5}
with

We have already given the brackets of the even elements. The
brackets of elements of
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{\bar{0}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{\bar{1}}
are as follows:

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}{}[V_{1},V_{4}]:&[\omega,\ \alpha\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v]=\alpha\cdot\omega\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v\in V_{5};\\
{}[V_{2},V_{4}]:&[f\otimes A,\ \alpha\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v]=(f\alpha-df\wedge d\alpha)\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes Av\in V_{4}\oplus V_{5};\\
{}[V_{3},V_{4}]:&[D,\ \alpha\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v]=(D(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D\cdot\alpha+\frac{1}{2}d(\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D)\wedge d\alpha)\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v\in V_{4}\oplus V_{5};\\
{}[V_{1},V_{5}]&=0;\\
{}[V_{2},V_{5}]:&[f\otimes A,\ \omega\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v]=f\omega\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes Av\in V_{5};\\
{}[V_{3},V_{5}]:&[D,\ \omega\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v]=(L_{D}\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D\cdot\omega)\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits^{-1/2}\otimes v\in V_{5}.\end{array}

To describe in these terms the bracket of two odd elements, we resort to
the following natural identifications:

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\Omega^{2}(3)}{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.24112pto\hskip-0.04822ptl\/}}}\nolimits}\simeq{\mathfrak{vect}}(3):\hskip 8.50012pt\frac{\omega}{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.24112pto\hskip-0.04822ptl\/}}}\nolimits}\longleftrightarrow D_{\omega}:\\
i_{D_{\omega}}(\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.34445pto\hskip-0.0689ptl\/}}}\nolimits)=\omega,\text{ ~{i.e.},~ }\sum f_{i}dx_{j}\wedge dx_{k}\longleftrightarrow\sum f_{i}\partial_{i_{i}};\\
\Lambda^{2}{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}:\;v\wedge w\longleftrightarrow\det\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}&w_{1}\\
v_{2}&w_{2}\end{pmatrix};\\
S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})\simeq{\mathfrak{sl}}(2):\;v\cdot w\longleftrightarrow\begin{pmatrix}-v_{1}w_{2}-v_{2}w_{1}&2v_{1}w_{1}\\
-2v_{2}w_{2}&v_{1}w_{2}+v_{2}w_{1}\end{pmatrix}.\end{array}

The bracket of two odd elements is of the form:

(261)

\begin{array}[]{ll}{}[V_{4},V_{4}]:&\left[\frac{f}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes v,\frac{g}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes w\right]=\frac{df\wedge dg\otimes v\wedge w}{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}\in{\mathfrak{vect}}(3)=V_{3};\\
{}[V_{5},V_{5}]:&\left[\frac{\omega_{1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes v,\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes w\right]=(D_{\omega_{1}}(\omega_{2})-\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D_{\omega_{1}}\cdot\omega_{2})v\wedge w\in V_{1};\\
{}[V_{4},V_{5}]:&\left[\frac{f}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes v,\frac{\omega}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}\otimes w\right]=\\
&\frac{f\omega}{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}\otimes v\wedge w-\frac{1}{2}(fd\omega-\omega\wedge df)\otimes v\cdot w+df\wedge d(\mathop{\text{\rm div}}\nolimits D_{\omega})\otimes v\wedge w.\end{array}

In the last line above, the first summand lies in
V_{3}
, the second
one in
V_{2}
, and the third one in
V_{1}
.
The difference as
compared with

[
CCK
]
: the coefficient of the third summand in
the last line on (
261
) should be
1
, whereas in
[
CCK
]

it is equal to
\frac{1}{2}
.

To verify, compute the Jacobi identity (it holds for 1 and not for

\frac{1}{2}
) for the triple

u_{3}du_{2}\wedge du_{3}\in V_{1}\text{~and~ $\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{u_{1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{u_{1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{u_{1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{u_{1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}\otimes e_{1}$,
$\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{u_{2}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{0.25pt}{$\dfrac{u_{2}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.43057pto\hskip-0.08612ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{u_{2}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{$\tfrac{u_{2}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{{v\hskip-0.3014pto\hskip-0.06029ptl\/}}}\nolimits}}$}}\otimes e_{2}\in V_{4}$, where $e_{1},e_{2}$ span
${\mathbb{C}}^{2}$.}\hskip 56.9055pt\qed

.

{\mathfrak{g}}
has a grading operator

-invariant cocycles,

(when

), but
they do not contribute to cohomology.

One of the Cartan

over
{\mathbb{C}}
is
\footnotesize{\begin{pmatrix}2&-1&0\\

\end{array}

{\mathfrak{der}}~\ {\mathfrak{psl}}(2|2)\simeq{\mathfrak{osp}}_{-1}(4|2)
and on the

structure of the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{sl}}(2)
